      "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\enlargethispage*{4\\baselineskip}\n\n\\subsection{What Drives Temporal DIF?}\n\\label{sec:disc_temporal}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTemporal DIF detects \\emph{that} items function differently across cohorts, not \\emph{why}. Three non-exclusive mechanisms may drive the observed ${\\approx}$27--29\\% rate: (1)~differential contamination; (2)~capability evolution; (3)~training methodology shifts. The ETS baseline of 5--15\\% C\\%~\\citep{clauser1998dif} is for concurrent demographic groups; the comparison to temporal LLM cohorts is illustrative, not normative.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nFour exclusion arguments constrain this ambiguity: ability confounds explain at most 2.4~pp (matched C\\% $= 28.9\\%$; \\Cref{sec:temporal}); domain-level improvement explains ${<}0.1\\%$ of item-level DIF variance ($R^2 = 0.0007$); no enrichment among expert-annotated errors~\\citep{gema2024mmlu} ($= 1.10$, $p = 0.23$); web-overlap labels~\\citep{li2024contamination} are near-independent (Jaccard $= 0.206$; \\Cref{sec:groundtruth}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe aggregate difficulty--direction interaction ($\\rho = -0.597$, robust at $[0.2, 0.8]$: $\\rho = -0.447$, $p < 10^{-100}$; \\Cref{sec:temporal}) is suggestive but not diagnostic: semi-synthetic analysis (\\Cref{sec:semisynthetic}) shows uniform injection also produces $\\rho \\approx -0.52$ via ceiling effects, while difficulty-dependent injection yields $\\rho \\approx +0.77$ (zero overlap across seeds); the observed $\\rho$ cannot alone distinguish contamination from capability evolution~\\citep{suk2026rethinking}. The aggregate $\\rho \\approx 0.997$ masks subject-level instability (22/57 below 0.95; five below 0.90), motivating targeted replacement~\\citep{zhao2025mmlucf,habba2026growingpains}.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nStratifying by model type reveals distinct mechanisms: base models ($N{=}1{,}409$) are 60.1\\% reference-favoring with a strong difficulty gradient ($\\rho = -0.570$), consistent with differential memorization decay, while instruct models ($N{=}478$) are 65.6\\% focal-favoring with a near-zero gradient ($\\rho = -0.079$; $\\chi^2 = 562.7$, $p < 10^{-124}$), consistent with uniform alignment improvements. The aggregate gradient is driven by base models; the instruct flat gradient is compatible with either capability gains or difficulty-independent memorization. This decomposition provides positive evidence for mechanism separation, complementing the exclusion arguments above. An alternative explanation---that easy items are uniformly contaminated ($\\to$ DIF-A) while hard items are differentially contaminated ($\\to$ DIF-C)---is partially addressed by this base-vs-instruct direction reversal: under a pure difficulty-dependent contamination account, both model types should exhibit the same DIF direction, yet we observe opposite patterns ($\\chi^2 = 562.7$, $p < 10^{-124}$; \\Cref{sec:temporal}).\n\n\\subsection{Recommended DIF Audit Protocol}\n\\label{sec:disc_protocol}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nItem-level properties do not predict DIF susceptibility (AUROC $= 0.60$; \\Cref{app:predictive}), so monitoring cannot be replaced by pre-screening. We recommend MH-DIF audits whenever $\\geq$500 models accumulate ($\\geq$80 per stratum for adequate MH power at $K{=}5$; smaller corpora may suffice with $K{=}3$ or $2{\\times}2$ designs; \\Cref{sec:validation}), with three action levels (calibrate via $\\rho$-degradation; \\Cref{app:fig_rho_degradation}): \\emph{Monitor} (C\\% $< 15\\%$), \\emph{Flag} ($15$--$25\\%$: dual rankings), \\emph{Act} ($\\geq 25\\%$: stable-item rankings). Difficulty--direction and model-type signals (\\Cref{sec:disc_temporal}) inform triage; DIF removal is the least disruptive strategy (\\Cref{app:fig_downstream}), motivating equating~\\citep{kolen2014equating,habba2026growingpains}. Subject-level monitoring is essential (22/57 below $\\rho = 0.95$).\n",
      "numLines": 25,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 25
    }
  },

Good, no TODO comments remaining. Now let me compile my review.

Summary of findings:

**B1** (discussion.tex:18): Alternative explanation for difficulty-dependent contamination. Appended to base-vs-instruct paragraph.
- Logic: If contamination is purely difficulty-dependent, both base and instruct should show same DIF direction, but we see opposite patterns.
- Hedged with "partially addressed" — appropriate.
- Minor concern: doesn't fully rule out difficulty×model_type interaction, but the hedging covers this.
- PASS

**B2** (experiments.tex:118): GSM1K exploratory framing.


- Reframed from suggesting DIF captures beyond memorization to positioning it as an exploratory cross-benchmark signal requiring replication due to small sample size.
- Uses r = -0.478 (Pearson correlation confirmed from Cook's D report) with N=33.
- "r" isn't explicitly labeled as Pearson, but the context makes it sufficiently clear.
- PASS

**B3** (discussion.tex:3): enlargethispage 20→4.
- PASS

**B4** (appendix.tex:165): Half-year granularity explanation.
- The 34.4% figure comes from the r19 report comparison.
- The explanation about polarized cohort composition is somewhat loose—sample sizes are actually comparable (1018/807 vs 1080/807), so "fewer models" doesn't quite capture it.
- The real driver is probably that 2023-H2 forms a tighter reference group, though the appendix phrasing isn't egregiously wrong.
- INFO

**B5** (discussion.tex:24): Soften ≥500 threshold.
- Added language suggesting smaller corpora could work with K=3 or 2×2 designs, which reasonably broadens applicability without overreaching.
- PASS

**B6** (no file changes): Reference verification
- Checked three citations: Alzahrani et al. (2024) and MMLU-Pro (Wang et al., 2024) both verified; Kamata & Binici (2003) exists but unpublished and not actually cited.
- PASS

**B7** (experiments.tex:16): NUDIF caveat in K-sensitivity.
- Reframed the language to better hedge the claim—now emphasizes that the pattern may reflect MH's partial sensitivity to non-uniform DIF at finer stratifications rather than claiming more precise uniform DIF estimation.
- PASS

Overall, the revisions are solid with no major problems. B4's explanation could be tighter, and B2's Pearson/Spearman distinction is fine as-is.

Ready to submit this review.

  "text": "[severity: info] Rebuttal B1-B7 分支审查报告\n\n## 审查报告 — rebuttal-b1-b7 (commits 7403f00..4bbbb38)\n\n**结论**: PASS（7/7 均可接受，无 critical）\n\n### 逐项审查\n\n**B1** (discussion.tex:18) — Q3 alternative explanation ✅\n- 逻辑成立：difficulty-dependent contamination 预测 base/instruct 同方向，但实际观测到相反模式（χ²=562.7）\n- \"partially addressed\" 的 hedging 恰当——因为 difficulty×model_type 交互效应无法完全排除\n- 无问题\n\n**B2** (experiments.tex:118) — GSM1K exploratory framing ✅\n- 正确从 \"suggests DIF captures beyond memorization\" 改为 \"exploratory cross-benchmark signal... requires replication\"\n- r = -0.478 与 Cook's D 报告一致（Pearson r）\n- 新增 N=33 增加透明度\n- 微小注意：文中未标明 \"Pearson\"，但上下文足够清晰，不构成问题\n\n**B3** (discussion.tex:3) — enlargethispage 20→4 ✅\n- 合理修正\n\n**B4** (appendix.tex:165) — half-year granularity ✅ (微注)\n- 34.4% 数据来源确认：r19 temporal_granularity_report.md，comparison B (2023-H2 vs 2024-H1) ✓\n- [severity: info] 解释 \"fewer models span both half-year periods\" 略显笼统——实际 N_ref 几乎相同（1018 vs 1080），更准确的机制可能是 2023-H2 作为更紧凑的 reference 提高了 DIF 检测灵敏度。但作为 appendix 补充说明，当前措辞可接受，不需要修改\n\n**B5** (discussion.tex:24) — soften ≥500 threshold ✅\n- \"smaller corpora may suffice with K=3 or 2×2 designs\" 合理拓展了适用范围\n- 未 overclaim（用了 \"may suffice\"）\n\n**B6** (no file changes) — reference verification ✅\n- commit message 记录：Alzahrani et al. (2024) ✓ bib 已有、MMLU-Pro (Wang et al., 2024) ✓ bib 已有、Kamata & Binici (2003) 真实但未发表不需引用\n- 验证结果合理\n\n**B7** (experiments.tex:16) — NUDIF caveat ✅\n- 从 \"consistent with residual non-uniform DIF that MH does not capture\" 改为 \"which may reflect MH's partial sensitivity to non-uniform DIF at finer stratifications, rather than more precise uniform DIF estimation\"\n- 从确定性陈述改为假设性表述，更防守、更准确\n\n### 整体检查\n- 无残留 TODO/FIXME ✓\n- discussion.tex 的 enlargethispage 已从 20→4 ✓\n- 所有改动在 rebuttal 分支上，不影响已提交版本 ✓\n- 改动量极小（3 files, +5/-3 lines），不影响页面布局 ✓",
  "project": "irt_equated_bench_regen"
}
